--- Commit Message ---
LU-19921 ptlrpc: register gssiam policy

This is the final patch in the GSSIAM (Identity and Access
Management) security flavor series. It calls
sptlrpc_register_policy(&sptlrpc_gssiam_policy) in
sptlrpc_gssiam_init() and sptlrpc_unregister_policy() in
sptlrpc_gssiam_exit() to enable the GSSIAM policy for client/server
authentication and authorization.

It also improves error-unwind handling in sptlrpc_gssiam_init()
by introducing out-of-line tunables_cleanup and cache_cleanup
labels after policy registration.

Signed-off-by: Di Wang <ddiwang@google.com>
Change-Id: Ic5e1bc4f7d0db18abcc7f52525d4a8e0efdae7c3

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-08-06 19:33:35.000000000) ---
Patch Set 71:

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Mostly good. The init/exit unwind is balanced and the ordering is right: register last, unregister first, and gssiam_tunables_fini() before upcall_cache_cleanup() so sysfs_remove_group() has drained the gssiam_upcall/downcall/flush handlers before the cache they dereference goes away.

One gap: this is the patch that makes the gssiam flavor selectable, and nothing under lustre/tests/ mentions gssiam. The tree already ships mock helpers built for exactly this - l_gssiam_auth accepts any token starting with "mock_gssiam_token" and l_gssiam_upcall retrieves it - but no test uses them. sanity-sec test_300 only checks that the user_principal/disable_rootsquash mount options parse. Could this add a test that sets the flavor to gssiam (sanity-krb5.sh's set_flavor_all/wait_flavor pattern) and exercises a mount and some IO over it, so the SEC_CTX_INIT handshake, the Null-framed data path and the dynamic nodemap creation get covered?
